   286	Do not publish a generic mutation accepting a document id and inserting a reader: that would bypass token enrollment. Mutations are the app's API; `insert <- .principal = viewer` alone does not prove token possession.
   287	
   288	## Split-table anonymity
   289	
   290	Private author attribution belongs in its own table; the published payload has no author field. Public replies and private audit rows are linked by id, so operators may still correlate them. This is application anonymity from other readers, not a claim about network logs or the store administrator.
   291	
   292	```quarry
   293	table responses:
   294	  body: text 1..200
   295	  public 'anonymous response bodies'
   296	  insert <- allow
   297	  online only
   298	table attribution:
   299	  response: responses
   300	  principal: principal
   301	  unique response
   302	  immutable response, principal
   303	  read <- .principal = viewer
   304	  insert <- .principal = viewer and created responses[.response]
   305	  online only
   306	mutation respond(body: text 1..200):
   307	  r = insert responses { body }
   308	  insert attribution { response: r.id, principal: viewer }
   309	  return r
   310	query author(response: responses):
   311	  return attribution[response = response]
   312	query response(response: responses):
   313	  return responses[response]
   314	```
   315	
   316	## Versioned media: mutable publication pointer
   317	
   318	Keep the band's member roster, immutable version audience and media references separate. A publication pointer chooses the released version. Here subscribers may read only the current version; moving the pointer immediately removes the old version's read grant. Keep it online: a mutable publication condition is not an invertible sync audience. The owner retains draft access. Downloads also need the version row's read authority (`guide assets`).
   319	
   320	```quarry
   321	table bands:
   322	  owner: principal
   323	  immutable owner
   324	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists fans[.id, viewer]
   325	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   326	  online only
   327	table fans:
   328	  band: bands
   329	  principal: principal
   330	  unique band, principal
   331	  read <- .principal = viewer or exists bands[.band, owner = viewer]
   332	  insert <- exists bands[.band, owner = viewer]
   333	  online only
   334	table versions:
   335	  band: bands
   336	  owner: principal
   337	  title: text 1..80
   338	  cover: image <=5000000 ?
   339	  immutable band, owner
   340	  read <- .owner = viewer or (exists fans[.band, viewer] and exists publications[version = .id])
   341	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists bands[.band, owner = viewer]
   342	  online only
   343	table publications:
   344	  band: bands
   345	  version: versions
   346	  unique band
   347	  unique version
   348	  read <- exists bands[.band, owner = viewer] or exists fans[.band, viewer]
   349	  insert <- exists bands[.band, owner = viewer] and exists versions[.version, band = .band]
   350	  update <- exists bands[.band, owner = viewer] and exists versions[.version, band = .band]
   351	  online only
   352	mutation publish(band: bands, version: versions):
   353	  return upsert publications[band = band] { version }
   354	query version(version: versions):
   355	  return versions[version]
   356	```
   357	
   358	## Mine, shared with my groups, and public
   359	
   360	A public branch plus private audiences stays online. One live read rule admits owners, public entries and team members. Three complete cursor pages serve Mine/Public/Team tabs; keep their cursors separate and deduplicate ids if combining them in the UI. An owner may make an entry public deliberately. A synced variant splits public entries into a public table and keeps owner-or-one-team rows separately (`guide sharing`); a union may contain at most one group audience.
   361	
   362	```quarry
   363	use identity
   364	
   365	table teams:
   366	  owner: principal
   367	  immutable owner
   368	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists members[.id, viewer]
   369	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   370	  online only
   371	table members:
   372	  team: teams
   373	  principal: principal
   374	  unique team, principal
   375	  immutable team, principal
   376	  read <- .principal = viewer
   377	  insert <- exists teams[.team, owner = viewer]
   378	  online only
   379	table library:
   380	  owner: principal
   381	  team: teams
   382	  shared: bool default false
   383	  title: text 1..80
   384	  immutable owner, team
   385	  read <- .owner = viewer or .shared = true or exists members[.team, viewer]
   386	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists teams[.team, owner = viewer]
   387	  update <- .owner = viewer
   388	  online only
   389	query mine(c: cursor ?):
   390	  return library where .owner = viewer first 20 by .id after c
   391	query publicLibrary(c: cursor ?):
   392	  return library where .shared = true first 20 by .id after c
   393	query teamLibrary(team: teams, c: cursor ?):
   394	  require exists members[team, viewer] else NOT_MEMBER
   395	  return library where .team = team first 20 by .id after c
   396	query entry(entry: library):
   397	  return library[entry]
   398	```
   399	
   400	Public visitors still need transport authentication: declare `use identity`, call `auth.guest()` from `snapback4/react`'s `useAuth()` (or `client.guest()`), then query. Public describes row authority, not an unauthenticated HTTP door. A guest can see shared rows, not team rows; password accounts enroll in the team roster. Use `use identity password` when the app should have no visitors.
   401	
   402	## Day-scoped and time-window visibility
   403	
   404	Store the assigned day as a key in a roster; select it in the API instead of
   405	putting a clock in a rule. A publication's lower and upper lifetime bounds are
   406	server enforced. Here staff assign readers, and only the assigned day reader
   407	can read the publication while it is live. Changing the roster revokes access
   408	on the next read; the day never changes on an existing publication.
   409	
   410	```quarry
   411	table dayReaders:
   412	  principal: principal
   413	  dayNumber: int
   414	  unique principal, dayNumber
   415	  immutable principal, dayNumber
   416	  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
   417	  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
   418	  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
   419	  online only
   420	table dayPublications:
   421	  dayNumber: int
   422	  opensAt: time
   423	  endsAt: time
   424	  body: text 1..200
   425	  by byOpening: opensAt
   426	  by byExpiry: endsAt
   427	  immutable dayNumber, opensAt, endsAt, body
   428	  not before .opensAt
   429	  expire at .endsAt
   430	  read <- exists dayReaders[viewer, .dayNumber]
   431	  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
   432	  online only
   433	mutation assignDay(who: principal, dayNumber: int):
   434	  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
   435	  return insert dayReaders { principal: who, dayNumber }
   436	mutation publishDay(dayNumber: int, opensAt: time, endsAt: time, body: text 1..200):
   437	  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
   438	  require opensAt < endsAt else BAD_WINDOW
   439	  return insert dayPublications { dayNumber, opensAt, endsAt, body }
   440	query dayPublication(publication: dayPublications):
   441	  return dayPublications[publication]
   442	```
   443	
   444	For a slot-relative audience (the previous speaker), store an immutable slot
   445	identity and a validated successor principal, then authorize that principal
   446	explicitly. A lookup of a hidden prior slot cannot repair its successor. Give
   447	that maintenance operation readable authority, or update by key under an
   448	explicit transition rule; do not silently skip a nullable hidden lookup.
   449	
   450	## Column-level privacy: public identity, private sheet
   451	
   452	A returned shape does not protect a stored field. Split the row so that every
   453	field in the public identity is public and the private sheet has its own read
   454	rule. Column-level read rules remain an open language decision; this recipe applies ordinary row rules.
   455	
   456	```quarry
   457	table identities:
   458	  principal: principal
   459	  name: text 1..80
   460	  unique principal
   461	  immutable principal
   462	  public 'display name and account principal are public'
   463	  insert <- .principal = viewer
   464	  online only
   465	table privateSheets:
   466	  identity: identities
   467	  owner: principal
   468	  notes: text 1..200
   469	  unique identity
   470	  immutable identity, owner
   471	  read <- .owner = viewer
   472	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists identities[.identity, principal = .owner]
   473	  online only
   474	mutation createIdentity(name: text 1..80, notes: text 1..200):
   475	  i = insert identities { principal: viewer, name }
   476	  insert privateSheets { identity: i.id, owner: viewer, notes }
   477	  return i
   478	query identity(identity: identities):
   479	  return identities[identity]
   480	query privateSheet(identity: identities):
   481	  return privateSheets[identity = identity]
   482	```
   483	
   484	## Status dashboard over private rows
   485	
   486	Declare the wider count disclosure on the view, not on private source rows.
   487	Role queries read the caller's own roster row; use them for access UI even
   488	when a private list is empty. An authorized missing count group is zero; a
   489	hidden keyed count is null. A roster holder can see the status count but not
   490	another user's body. Counts disclose activity; grant them deliberately.
   491	
   492	```quarry

   494	  principal: principal
   495	  unique principal
   496	  read <- .principal = viewer
   497	  online only
   498	table privateTickets:
   499	  owner: principal
   500	  status: enum('open', 'closed')
   501	  body: text 1..200
   502	  immutable owner
   503	  read <- .owner = viewer
   504	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   505	  update <- .owner = viewer
   506	  online only
   507	view statusCounts: count privateTickets by .status
   508	  read <- exists dashboardStaff[viewer]
   509	query dashboardRole():
   510	  return exists dashboardStaff[viewer]

   514	  return privateTickets[ticket]
   515	mutation ticket(body: text 1..200):
   516	  return insert privateTickets { owner: viewer, status: 'open', body }
   517	```
   518	
   519	## Audience follows a mutable parent
   520	
   521	Copy-at-insert plus immutable copies keeps the creation-time audience, so a
   522	transfer leaves existing children with the old owner. `read through` captures
   523	a parent's immutable audience inputs and lifetime; it refuses mutable inputs
   524	and does not follow owner transfers. A fixed stricter audience (for example
   525	the child's author) intentionally never follows the parent. To follow a mutable
   526	parent live, stay online and test its current owner in every read. This recipe
   527	transfers existing children with the parent; the original author loses access.

 succeeded in 0ms:
## 4. Fixture intent and guard visibility

`check` inspects shared cases only for operations declared by the checked
schema. Signature/decoding/validation failures whose runtime code is E_INPUT
are intentional when `expect` equals E_INPUT, so emit no argument-drift note.
Malformed JSON/case metadata remain authoring errors. Unknown operations
remain a hard error in `test`, including misspellings; read-only `check`
intentionally cannot distinguish shared newer operations from misspellings.
Expectations are the existing public refusal code, without a new phase field;
the runner prints the E_INPUT message/subcause on each passing negative case
so a negative assertion is reviewable without introducing another note. Saved runtime values
remain unknown. The fixture runner recognizes decoding/signature errors as
E_INPUT too; tests cannot claim success by skipping the case.

A program `exists` sees only caller-readable rows; a table-rule probe has
authority over all rows. Keep those semantics. Add N_UNREADABLE_PROBE on
negative requirements and conditional guards over private populations unless
the pins/preceding guards prove readability. Positive `require exists` is
itself an authorization check; retain its existing N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN analysis.
To cover values later used through aliases/returned helper booleans, inspect
point-existence observations when they happen, as well as mutation/job prefix
observations. Pure query prefix/search/seek values remain ordinary visible-hit
answers; trace guard-consumed bindings through aliases/objects/possible rebinding
and check those observations with their original authority. Inputs to a
guard-consuming helper/shape are conservatively tracked too (all inputs,
rather than an unsound partial parameter-use proof), including direct expression
inputs and guarded loop domains. Row-dependent candidate filters remain intentional
visible-hit query predicates (global gates lower to whole-page conditionals
and are checked); a computed boolean field passed to a guard is
still an observation. Snapshot markers exclude names in every actual argument,
so postorder substitution cannot match a fabricated name in another argument.
Helpers/shapes reached from a requirement inspect every direct input value,
including helpers that only invert it in their returned expression. A direct
positive `require exists` remains authorization. Traverse requirement
wrappers (members, objects, calls and nested conditionals), preserving direct
positive-existence authorization. Inline shape bases are inspected independently
of named-definition discovery. Expand object/write shorthand in analysis copies
before classifying global facts, discovering effects, checking shadowing, or
excluding/substituting/validating argument names; shorthand and explicit names
must have the same scope and proof behavior. Runtime expressions stay unchanged.
Name the target read
rule and explain that negative guards cannot enforce global absence. This is
advisory; an intentionally caller-visible guard can remain unchanged.

Follow reachable helpers, named shapes and spreads. Facts cross helper scopes
only through actual arguments or viewer/literals, with fresh intermediate names
to prevent caller/formal spelling collisions. A read-only helper can receive
snapshot evidence for an expression argument; a writing helper gets only scalar
names. Successful ruled physical-row lookups prove their unambiguous indexed
row exists, omitting extra projected-field tests. Viewer has one meaning in
user text and compiler-generated recipes. Every extra read-rule equality must
resolve; none may be dropped. Never borrow a positional index's visibility
proof from another same-width index. An explicit computed/unknown index never
falls back to another index, and computed keys are not raw column pins.

Dominating facts are invalidated by writes, relevant binding changes or viewer
scope changes; an unanalysed control-flow path never proves authority. Follow
helper/shape effects conservatively through the call graph; cycles prove
nothing. Potentially writing loops cannot carry entry facts across backedges
or export prior role evidence. Binding-only loops invalidate potentially rebound
names at entry/exit, including break/continue paths. An in-loop readable guard
can reacquire proof each iteration. Inspect domain conditionals too. Before
inspecting a compound expression that may write, drop prior facts because a
later operand can observe the changed world. A standalone helper call with
non-writing arguments instead analyzes the helper's statements in order, then
drops caller evidence if it wrote. Stop at return/break/continue. Viewer scopes
inside jobs use caller reads and receive the note; surrounding system job
guards remain exempt. Use a unique/cap/final constraint for an invariant across
hidden rows; do not recommend making private rows public. Avoid duplicate notes
at one guard/target. This never changes execution or admission.

## 5. Refusals and usable rewrites

 succeeded in 0ms:
## 7. Wave 11 diagnostic precision (QDIAG)

The nullable-reference lint counts a null value as needing no target lookup.
At `if arg != null`, only the null edge carries that exemption; a lookup on
the other edge must still dominate the write. Join by intersection and clear
facts on rebinding. This changes advisory output only.

Operation parameter defaults remain explicit: declare an optional parameter
and bind `value = arg ?? literal`. Refuse `= literal` with that exact idiom.
A multi-line `require` keeps `else CODE` on its closing line; the refusal names
that line and preserves the author's code. `?` precedes `on delete keep`.

Program absence checks on a row lookup (`t[key] = null`) use the unreadable
probe note too. Raw table-rule probes, including delete rules, still observe
all rows; the note's repair states that distinction. A later owner `require`
after `! else NOT_FOUND` gets a refusal-order note only when every read-rule
branch requires that same owner. A branch admitting staff or another party
can legitimately reach the custom refusal and keeps the note quiet.

A child-owner proof requires equality in every child insert branch, immutable
(or unupdatable) child owner/reference and parent owner, no maintained or
field-owned value, no opaque/job writer, no parent expiry (expiry fires no reference action), and no kept parent identity
that ordinary or cascading deletion could recreate,
and equal read rules after replacing
only that owner. No other row fields may appear in either rule. A successful
parent lookup (or a direct probe in its returned row projection) supplies the
parent-readable fact. This is advisory analysis of ordinary admitted writes;
trusted system seeds must preserve the declared application invariant. It never
changes read authority, admission or completeness.

## 8. Refused in the first version

Each is refused at `check` with a rewrite that keeps privacy, as `E_SYNC`
at the sync line except the aggregate cases, which the core's maintain
checks report (and the binder avoids by inheriting no union):

1. **Two group arms.** Needs arm-tagged group partitions (`g:` names would
   collide across arms) and per-arm held groups on the device for R2.
2. **A `public` arm.** That is B2 (LLP 3000.036 §1), with its own
   commit-boundary accounting.
3. **`retain delivered-history`** on a union table. Retained rows of a left
   group and current rows of a column arm would share the group's retention
   markers.
4. **`read through`** a union parent, or a union table as a read-through
   child. A child would need the parent's union with captured columns.
5. **A synced aggregate, a row-keyed view, or `with own keys`** over a
   union source (§5.4).
6. **A timeline** whose posts or reposts table has a union audience.
7. **Private field owners** (`written by` without `readable by audience`)
   on a union table: they add owner partitions that R2 would have to
   distinguish from column arms.
8. **Expiry on union content or a membership scope**, direct or inherited.
   The diagnostic names the scope with `expire at`; use `online only` to keep
   the read rule, lifetime and queries. This includes pure-column unions.
9. **Any union acquisition window**, and **a windowed membership scope or
   inherited ancestor**. The diagnostic names `last N by ...` or its source
   scope; use `online only`, or remove the union sync window to replicate all
   readable rows when no other excluded lifetime/window remains. Ordinary
   query pagination (`first`/`last`, cursors) remains supported.

This is the coordinator's conditional narrowing, triggered by round 10's
reproduced High D2 defect: a content row expires before Alice joins, the
snapshot omits it, and a later physical sweep nevertheless sends Alice its
id. We refuse that union source rather than adding another expiry path.
The broader single-audience mechanism predates unions and retains its current
contract; further expiry/window work is tracked in
[the follow-up ticket](../issues/20261012-union-expiry-windows-deferred.md).


LLP 3000.036 §1 B1 (an explicit `sync to` naming one whole disjunct, the
rest answered online) was recommended there but never built, so refusing
`sync to .owner` over an owner-or-team rule removes nothing that compiled:
before this RFC that line was refused as a disagreement, and it still is,
now with the union line as its rewrite. The union serves every reader B1
would have served and the ones it would have sent online (Muse finding 5,
answered).

## 8a. Deferred

- **Each exclusion of §8**, with the reason given there. In particular, an
  expiring group arm, including inherited expiry, remains refused on unions;
  the renewal/expiry/sweep traces cover the admitted single-group path.
- **Union audiences on the PostgreSQL evaluation adapter** (§9).
- **Optional broader simulator reach:** exhaustive depth 3 remains opt-in;
  blocking targeted tests now cover epoch leave→rejoin→create,
  rebirth chains and roster/team cross-table interleavings. The exhaustive
  worlds use memory devices; the named traces and snapshot page-size sweep
  additionally apply to SQLite. No windowed union is admitted or claimed.
  Predictions racing scopes remain covered by the general device suite,
  rather than a UNION-specific exhaustive world.

The former pinned-group page deferral is resolved by the integrated page
guards: an index prefix that pins a self-disclosing membership probe can
establish a successful branch for every row, so the server can return a
complete page. See the [closed ticket](../issues/closed/20261011-snapback4-prefix-decided-grants-at-run-time.md).

Pre-revision-10 epoch records from this unreleased Draft have no activation
clock and retain their prior baseline behavior. The sequence alone cannot
reconstruct the activation clock: its commit may occur later. This is a bound
on old experimental stores, not a released-data migration claim. Optional
new coverage includes explicit-null lifetime scopes, UNION-specific prediction
races and snapshots straddling an audience activation.

 succeeded in 0ms:
### 3.2 Authorization and revocation

- Ephemeral rows are read under their table's read rule, like any row.
- Rules are evaluated at read time, and ephemeral rows are never cached on a
  device. So a principal who loses membership sees nothing on their next
  read of that room.
- A durable commit to a table a watched query probes invalidates that
  query, as today.
- By the renewal law (§2.2), a departed member's rows outlive the departure
  by at most one life, whatever the update rule says.
- **Disclosure.** The `/changes` answer names ephemeral tables that were
  written. That is the same class as today's table-granular durable
  invalidation, which names every table a commit touched (`serve_changes.rs:80-94`).
  It adds no new leak class. Capacity refusals depend only on the writer's
  own rows (§3.5), except for the global backstop, which reveals only a
  server-wide aggregate, like HTTP admission.

### 3.3 Change notification

+    ("N_WRITE_DENIED", "a mutation writes a table whose rule for that effect (or upsert arm) is deny or omitted, or whose update rule contradicts its preceding caller conditions, so no caller but the system can pass it and the write always refuses E_RULE (or keeps a counter on a table only a role may update, for callers who never hold it). Write the rule the callers need, keep the count as a maintained view, or do the write in a job, which runs as the system; guide rules."),
     ("N_PROBE_ONLY_READ", "a note, not an error — a read-rule disjunct probes a private table through the row's reference and names no caller, so it admits every signed-in caller while that row exists: a rule probe sees every row and inherits no read rule. Write `read through .ref`, or name the caller in the probe; guide rules."),
     ("N_VACUOUS_RULE", "a note, not an error — an insert rule every inserting mutation satisfies by writing `viewer` itself, on a row naming a principal the caller chooses, while another rule trusts that column: any caller can become that principal's counterpart. Test the role in the rule or `require` it; guide rules."),
     ("N_LOOKUP_HIDDEN", "a note, not an error — an update or delete rule admits a role no read-rule disjunct uses, and a mutation writes that table by key: the lookup runs under the read rule first, so a holder who reads the row no other way gets NOT_FOUND. Let the role read the rows it acts on; guide rules."),
@@ -242,7 +243,7 @@ pub const CODES: &[(&str, &str)] = &[
     ("E_FIELD", "family input — a field was read from a value that has none (a null row's column, a missing object field). Narrow the row first: `row ! else NOT_FOUND`."),
     ("E_SCHEMA", "family schema — the schema or the store is inconsistent. `snapback4 check` names the line."),
     ("E_SCHEMA_CONSTRAINT", "family schema — a declaration the compiler binds but the core refuses: a cap or an expire without its index, an aggregate view without its unique group index, a sync whose aggregate would reveal rows its audience may not read. The message names the declaration; guide indexes."),
-    ("E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT", "a mutation cannot safely read this population. See guide programs for admission and guide sequences for receipt-gap probes. The detailed card explains constraints, read authority and the job alternative."),
+    ("E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT", "a mutation cannot safely read this population. See guide programs for admission and guide sequences for receipt-gap probes.      ("E_UNBOUND_SCAN", "family schema — a compiled program scans a table or index the schema no longer has. Compiling and serving refuse it, and a native device checks again; the web device trusts that admission and does not repeat the check, so a hand-built or corrupted backend can still run there. Recompile: `snapback4 check` after the schema change."),

Trusted dev explains expiry or possible causes for unknown tokens; sign in again and discard saved tokens from earlier stores. Serve and untrusted dev requests keep token standing generic; SDKs retain the server message: send `x-snapback-persona` in development or a bearer token (a persona is refused off loopback and under `serve`); claiming effects and minting sessions take a service token (`snapback4 token --service <name>`), never a user's (guide services)."),
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/refusals.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/refusals.rs
index 4e925f21b5..7aa3d298c6 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/refusals.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/refusals.rs
@@ -20,6 +20,23 @@ The client adds link (a transport or HTTP response failure) and client (the clie
 Reads never raise a rule refusal: a
 hidden row is null, and a page omits it.
 
+## Client-visible refusal ladder
+
+For an operation written as decode → asserted lookup → write → require:
+
+| Rung | Client-visible answer |
+| --- | --- |
+| Argument decoding | `E_INPUT`; no statement ran |
+| Hidden or missing `t[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` | `NOT_FOUND`; a nullable `t[id]` is null and does not refuse |
+| Table write rule | `E_RULE` for a readable denied target; `NOT_FOUND` for a hidden update/delete target; hidden insert collisions stay `E_RULE` |
+| Program `require` | The authored `else CODE`, if execution reaches this statement |
+
+After decoding, authored statement order decides precedence: a require placed
+before the write can win, while a later require cannot replace an earlier
+lookup or write refusal. For enrolment without a hidden-parent lookup, see
+`guide rule-recipes --section "Enrolment into hidden things"`. Transport guest
+sessions and production authentication: SURF owns `guide personas`/`guide wire`.
+
 ## Which refusal fires first
 
 An operation stops at the first refusal it reaches, and a mutation's writes
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rule_recipes.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rule_recipes.md
index 7886b9a4af..2b65a302a4 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rule_recipes.md
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rule_recipes.md
@@ -398,3 +398,513 @@ query entry(entry: library):
 ```
 
 Public visitors still need transport authentication: declare `use identity`, call `auth.guest()` from `snapback4/react`'s `useAuth()` (or `client.guest()`), then query. Public describes row authority, not an unauthenticated HTTP door. A guest can see shared rows, not team rows; password accounts enroll in the team roster. Use `use identity password` when the app should have no visitors.
+
+## Day-scoped and time-window visibility
+
+Store the assigned day as a key in a roster; select it in the API instead of
+putting a clock in a rule. A publication's lower and upper lifetime bounds are
+server enforced. Here staff assign readers, and only the assigned day reader
+can read the publication while it is live. Changing the roster revokes access
+on the next read; the day never changes on an existing publication.
+
+```quarry
+table dayReaders:
+  principal: principal
+  dayNumber: int
+  unique principal, dayNumber
+  immutable principal, dayNumber
+  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  online only
+table dayPublications:
+  dayNumber: int
+  opensAt: time
+  endsAt: time
+  body: text 1..200
+  by byOpening: opensAt
+  by byExpiry: endsAt
+  immutable dayNumber, opensAt, endsAt, body
+  not before .opensAt
+  expire at .endsAt
+  read <- exists dayReaders[viewer, .dayNumber]
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  online only
+mutation assignDay(who: principal, dayNumber: int):
+  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  return insert dayReaders { principal: who, dayNumber }
+mutation publishDay(dayNumber: int, opensAt: time, endsAt: time, body: text 1..200):
+  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  require opensAt < endsAt else BAD_WINDOW
+  return insert dayPublications { dayNumber, opensAt, endsAt, body }
+query dayPublication(publication: dayPublications):
+  return dayPublications[publication]
+```
+
+For a slot-relative audience (the previous speaker), store an immutable slot
+identity and a validated successor principal, then authorize that principal
+explicitly. A lookup of a hidden prior slot cannot repair its successor. Give
+that maintenance operation readable authority, or update by key under an
+explicit transition rule; do not silently skip a nullable hidden lookup.
+
+## Column-level privacy: public identity, private sheet
+
+A returned shape does not protect a stored field. Split the row so that every
+field in the public identity is public and the private sheet has its own read
+rule. Column-level read rules remain an open language decision; this recipe applies ordinary row rules.
+
+```quarry
+table identities:
+  principal: principal
+  name: text 1..80
+  unique principal
+  immutable principal
+  public 'display name and account principal are public'
+  insert <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table privateSheets:
+  identity: identities
+  owner: principal
+  notes: text 1..200
+  unique identity
+  immutable identity, owner
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists identities[.identity, principal = .owner]
+  online only
+mutation createIdentity(name: text 1..80, notes: text 1..200):
+  i = insert identities { principal: viewer, name }
+  insert privateSheets { identity: i.id, owner: viewer, notes }
+  return i
+query identity(identity: identities):
+  return identities[identity]
+query privateSheet(identity: identities):
+  return privateSheets[identity = identity]
+```
+
+## Status dashboard over private rows
+
+Declare the wider count disclosure on the view, not on private source rows.
+Role queries read the caller's own roster row; use them for access UI even
+when a private list is empty. An authorized missing count group is zero; a
+hidden keyed count is null. A roster holder can see the status count but not
+another user's body. Counts disclose activity; grant them deliberately.
+
+```quarry
+table dashboardStaff:
+  principal: principal
+  unique principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table privateTickets:
+  owner: principal
+  status: enum('open', 'closed')
+  body: text 1..200
+  immutable owner
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  update <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+view statusCounts: count privateTickets by .status
+  read <- exists dashboardStaff[viewer]
+query dashboardRole():
+  return exists dashboardStaff[viewer]
+query statusCount(status: enum('open', 'closed')):
+  return { count: statusCounts[status] }
+query privateTicket(ticket: privateTickets):
+  return privateTickets[ticket]
+mutation ticket(body: text 1..200):
+  return insert privateTickets { owner: viewer, status: 'open', body }
+```
+
+## Audience follows a mutable parent
+
+Copy-at-insert plus immutable copies keeps the creation-time audience, so a
+transfer leaves existing children with the old owner. `read through` captures
+a parent's immutable audience inputs and lifetime; it refuses mutable inputs
+and does not follow owner transfers. A fixed stricter audience (for example
+the child's author) intentionally never follows the parent. To follow a mutable
+parent live, stay online and test its current owner in every read. This recipe
+transfers existing children with the parent; the original author loses access.
+
+```quarry
+table movableFolders:
+  owner: principal
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer
+  online only
+table liveFiles:
+  folder: movableFolders
+  body: text 1..200
+  immutable folder
+  read <- exists movableFolders[.folder, owner = viewer]
+  insert <- exists movableFolders[.folder, owner = viewer]
+  online only
+mutation newMovableFolder():
+  return insert movableFolders { owner: viewer }
+mutation liveFile(folder: movableFolders, body: text 1..200):
+  f = movableFolders[folder] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  return insert liveFiles { folder: f.id, body }
+mutation transferFolder(folder: movableFolders, who: principal):
+  f = movableFolders[folder] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  update movableFolders[f.id] { owner: who }
+  return null
+query liveFileById(file: liveFiles):
+  return liveFiles[file]
+```
+
+## Minimum-group publication
+
+A rule cannot count a population. Maintain a count, check its readable group,
+and publish a copy atomically. Approvals here are irrevocable, so publication
+is monotone. For retractable consent, withdraw the publication in the same
+transaction as every withdrawal, as in Materialised access; a later cleanup
+job leaves a disclosure window.
+
+```quarry
+table petitions:
+  owner: principal
+  body: text 1..200
+  immutable owner, body
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table approvals:
+  petition: petitions
+  principal: principal
+  unique petition, principal
+  immutable petition, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer or exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
+  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists petitions[.petition]
+  online only
+view approvalCount: count approvals by .petition
+  read <- exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
+table petitionPublications:
+  petition: petitions
+  body: text 1..200
+  unique petition
+  immutable petition, body
+  public 'the owner published after ten irrevocable approvals'
+  insert <- exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
+  online only
+mutation petition(body: text 1..200):
+  return insert petitions { owner: viewer, body }
+mutation approve(petition: petitions):
+  return insert approvals { petition, principal: viewer }
+mutation publishPetition(petition: petitions):
+  p = petitions[petition] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  require (approvalCount[petition] ?? 0) >= 10 else TOO_FEW
+  return insert petitionPublications { petition, body: p.body }
+query petitionPublication(petition: petitions):
+  return petitionPublications[petition = petition]
+```
+
+Only publish the trusted threshold mutation. The table rule proves the owner,
+not the threshold; adding a generic publication writer would bypass it.
+
+## Secret ballots, closed publication, maintained result
+
+Keep voter choices private, and disclose only the maintained result after
+closing. The public poll metadata makes the closed-phase probe explicit.
+Neither insert nor delete can change votes after closure. There is no reopen
+operation. A readable empty result is zero; while open the count key is hidden
+and reads null. This is choice privacy from other voters, not administrator
+anonymity. Small totals may reveal an individual choice.
+
+```quarry
+table polls:
+  owner: principal
+  closed: bool default false
+  immutable owner
+  public 'poll identity and whether voting is closed are public'
+  insert <- .owner = viewer and .closed = false
+  update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer and old.closed = false and next.closed = true
+  online only
+table ballots:
+  poll: polls
+  voter: principal
+  choice: enum('yes', 'no')
+  unique poll, voter
+  immutable poll, voter, choice
+  read <- .voter = viewer
+  insert <- .voter = viewer and exists polls[.poll, closed = false]
+  delete <- .voter = viewer and exists polls[.poll, closed = false]
+  online only
+view ballotResults: count ballots by .poll, .choice
+  read <- exists polls[.poll, closed = true]
+mutation poll():
+  return insert polls { owner: viewer }
+mutation vote(poll: polls, choice: enum('yes', 'no')):
+  return insert ballots { poll, voter: viewer, choice }
+mutation closePoll(poll: polls):
+  p = polls[poll] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  require p.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER
+  update polls[p.id] { closed: true }
+  return null
+query ballotResult(poll: polls, choice: enum('yes', 'no')):
+  return { count: ballotResults[poll, choice] }
+query ownBallot(poll: polls):
+  return ballots[poll, viewer]
+```
+
+## Ephemeral presence under an enrolment parent
+
+An ephemeral row cannot use `read through`. Copy the durable scope, verify it
+against the writer-readable enrolment on every write, and probe the durable
+roster for current read authority. Removing enrolment hides presence on the
+next read; the memory row need not be deleted first. Renewals also require
+membership. Never replace presence with a durable table to repair this refusal.
+
+```quarry
+table spaces:
+  title: text 1..80
+  public 'space metadata is public; participation is private'
+  online only
+table enrolments:
+  space: spaces
+  principal: principal
+  unique space, principal
+  immutable space, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  delete <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
+  online only
+table presenceRows:
+  enrolment: enrolments
+  space: spaces
+  principal: principal
+  unique space, principal
+  immutable enrolment, space, principal
+  read <- exists enrolments[.space, viewer]
+  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer] and .space = .enrolment.space
+  update <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer] and .space = .enrolment.space
+  ephemeral 4s
+mutation pulse(space: spaces):
+  m = enrolments[space, viewer] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  return upsert presenceRows[space, viewer] { enrolment: m.id }
+mutation leaveSpace(space: spaces):
+  delete enrolments[space, viewer]
+  return null
+query presence(space: spaces, who: principal):
+  return presenceRows[space, who]
+```
+
+## Blind comparison with a system job
+
+A mutation cannot scan or compare secrets its caller cannot read. A system job
+can read them, but must publish only the explicitly permitted derived fact.
+Here the operator schedules the comparison, and ordinary callers get only
+which of two known submissions is higher, not either score. Jobs run as the
+system; no client mutation delegates arbitrary secret comparisons.
+
+```quarry
+table secretScores:
+  owner: principal
+  score: int
+  immutable owner, score
+  read <- .owner = viewer
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table comparisons:
+  left: secretScores
+  right: secretScores
+  leftHigher: bool
+  public 'operator-authorized comparison discloses only the ordering'
+  online only
+mutation secretScore(score: int):
+  return insert secretScores { owner: viewer, score }
+job compare(left: secretScores, right: secretScores):
+  for a in secretScores where .id = left:
+    b = secretScores[right] ! else NOT_FOUND
+    insert comparisons { left: a.id, right, leftHigher: a.score > b.score }
+query comparison(comparison: comparisons):
+  return comparisons[comparison]
+query secretScoreById(score: secretScores):
+  return secretScores[score]
+```
+
+## Enrolment into hidden things: invite, self-join or public metadata
+
+Choose one entry contract. **Invite:** the inviter reads the private parent and
+creates an addressed invitation; the recipient reads that invitation, then
+inserts their own membership without looking up the hidden parent. The table
+insert rule probes the parent and invitation even when program reads cannot.
+**Self-join:** deliberately make the root metadata public and require its open
+flag in the membership insert rule. **Public metadata:** keep a separate public
+catalogue with no private payload, and let its entry operation create private
+membership. Knowing an id is not consent and does not make a hidden parent
+readable. `! else NOT_FOUND` before joining will stop the invitee too early.
+
+```quarry
+table hiddenClubs:
+  owner: principal
+  title: text 1..80
+  immutable owner
+  read <- .owner = viewer or exists clubMembers[.id, viewer]
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table clubInvites:
+  club: hiddenClubs
+  principal: principal
+  unique club, principal
+  immutable club, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer or exists hiddenClubs[.club, owner = viewer]
+  insert <- exists hiddenClubs[.club, owner = viewer]
+  delete <- .principal = viewer
+  online only
+table clubMembers:
+  club: hiddenClubs
+  principal: principal
+  unique club, principal
+  immutable club, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists hiddenClubs[.club] and exists clubInvites[.club, viewer]
+  online only
+mutation club(title: text 1..80):
+  return insert hiddenClubs { owner: viewer, title }
+mutation inviteClub(club: hiddenClubs, who: principal):
+  c = hiddenClubs[club] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  return insert clubInvites { club: c.id, principal: who }
+mutation acceptClub(club: hiddenClubs):
+  i = clubInvites[club, viewer] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  m = insert clubMembers { club: i.club, principal: viewer }
+  delete clubInvites[i.id]
+  return m
+query hiddenClub(club: hiddenClubs):
+  return hiddenClubs[club]
+```
+
+For the self-join variant, replace the private root read rule with explicit
+public metadata and the membership insert rule with `.principal = viewer and
+exists root[.root, open = true]`; there is no invite. Do not apply that variant
+to a synced closed roster: its grant law also requires existing membership or
+a newly created parent (`guide sharing`). SURF owns transport authentication
+and production UI recipes; KIT owns proof-kit details in `guide testing`.
+
+## Revocable media links with expiry
+
+Redemption copies the link's upper lifetime to a principal grant. A live grant
+permits the media row and its download; an expired grant is absent even before
+physical sweep. Tokens are unguessable secrets generated outside Quarry. Keep
+them out of exported query results and listings. Revoking a token prevents new
+redemptions; revoke existing readers separately to remove their media access.
+Revocation cannot retract downloaded bytes. The asset itself is placed on the
+private row through the ordinary upload API (`guide assets`).
+
+```quarry
+table mediaDocuments:
+  owner: principal
+  title: text 1..80
+  image: image <=5000000 ?
+  immutable owner
+  read <- .owner = viewer or exists mediaReaders[.id, viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  update <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table mediaLinks:
+  secret: text 32..100
+  document: mediaDocuments
+  expiresAt: time
+  unique secret
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  immutable secret, document, expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  read <- allow
+  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  online only
+table mediaReaders:
+  document: mediaDocuments
+  principal: principal
+  expiresAt: time
+  unique document, principal
+  by byExpiry: expiresAt
+  immutable document, principal, expiresAt
+  expire at .expiresAt
+  read <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
+  insert <- .principal = viewer
+  delete <- viewer = 'service:ops'
+  online only
+mutation mediaDocument(title: text 1..80):
+  return insert mediaDocuments { owner: viewer, title }
+mutation placeMedia(document: mediaDocuments, image: image <=5000000):
+  update mediaDocuments[document] { image }
+  return null
+mutation mediaLink(document: mediaDocuments, secret: text 32..100, expiresAt: time):
+  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  d = mediaDocuments[document] ! else NOT_FOUND
+  require expiresAt > now else EXPIRED_LINK
+  return insert mediaLinks { document: d.id, secret, expiresAt }
+mutation claimMedia(secret: text 32..100):
+  t = mediaLinks[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
+  return insert mediaReaders { document: t.document, principal: viewer, expiresAt: t.expiresAt }
+mutation revokeMediaLink(secret: text 32..100):
+  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  t = mediaLinks[secret = secret] ! else BAD_LINK
+  delete mediaLinks[t.id]
+  return null
+mutation revokeMediaReader(document: mediaDocuments, who: principal):
+  require viewer = 'service:ops' else NOT_OPERATOR
+  delete mediaReaders[document = document, principal = who]
+  return null
+query mediaDocumentById(document: mediaDocuments):
+  return mediaDocuments[document]
+```
+
+Only expose claimMedia as the reader writer. A generic insert of a grant would
+bypass possession and expiry checks. Repeated claims use the declared unique
+constraint; they do not extend a grant's expiry.
+
+## N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE
+
+N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE means a nullable point lookup is always null for callers
+satisfying its preceding require/branch conditions: those conditions contradict
+the table read rule, so a non-null branch cannot run. Authorize the read or
+write by key under the table rule; Enrolment into hidden things shows the latter.
+Unknown relationships and role overlap do not trigger this proof.
+
+### Self-join through a public catalogue
+
+The public catalogue is the root metadata; the private payload has a separate
+row rule. Open entries permit self-join without an invitation. Closed entries
+refuse the join even though their metadata is readable. Opening an entry is
+an explicit owner decision, not a consequence of knowing its id.
+
+```quarry
+table clubCatalogue:
+  owner: principal
+  title: text 1..80
+  open: bool
+  immutable owner
+  public 'club titles and whether enrolment is open are public metadata'
+  insert <- .owner = viewer
+  update <- .owner = viewer
+  online only
+table catalogueMembers:
+  club: clubCatalogue
+  principal: principal
+  unique club, principal
+  immutable club, principal
+  read <- .principal = viewer
+  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, open = true]
+  online only
+table clubSheets:
+  club: clubCatalogue
+  owner: principal
+  body: text 1..200
+  unique club
+  immutable club, owner
+  read <- .owner = viewer or exists catalogueMembers[.club, viewer]
+  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, owner = .owner]
+  online only
+mutation catalogueClub(title: text 1..80, open: bool, body: text 1..200):
+  c = insert clubCatalogue { owner: viewer, title, open }
+  insert clubSheets { club: c.id, owner: viewer, body }
+  return c
+mutation selfJoinClub(club: clubCatalogue):
+  return insert catalogueMembers { club, principal: viewer }
+query catalogueClubById(club: clubCatalogue):
+  return clubCatalogue[club]
+query clubSheet(club: clubCatalogue):
+  return clubSheets[club = club]
+```
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/guide/rules.md
index f5df52134d..a81772390a 100644

   519	## Audience follows a mutable parent
   520	
   521	Copy-at-insert plus immutable copies keeps the creation-time audience, so a
   522	transfer leaves existing children with the old owner. `read through` captures
   523	a parent's immutable audience inputs and lifetime; it refuses mutable inputs
   524	and does not follow owner transfers. A fixed stricter audience (for example
   525	the child's author) intentionally never follows the parent. To follow a mutable
   526	parent live, stay online and test its current owner in every read. This recipe
   527	transfers existing children with the parent; the original author loses access.
   528	
   529	```quarry
   530	table movableFolders:
   531	  owner: principal
   532	  read <- .owner = viewer
   533	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   534	  update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer
   535	  online only
   536	table liveFiles:
   537	  folder: movableFolders
   538	  body: text 1..200
   539	  immutable folder
   540	  read <- exists movableFolders[.folder, owner = viewer]
   541	  insert <- exists movableFolders[.folder, owner = viewer]
   542	  online only
   543	mutation newMovableFolder():
   544	  return insert movableFolders { owner: viewer }
   545	mutation liveFile(folder: movableFolders, body: text 1..200):
   546	  f = movableFolders[folder] ! else NOT_FOUND
   547	  return insert liveFiles { folder: f.id, body }
   548	mutation transferFolder(folder: movableFolders, who: principal):
   549	  f = movableFolders[folder] ! else NOT_FOUND
   550	  update movableFolders[f.id] { owner: who }
   551	  return null
   552	query liveFileById(file: liveFiles):
   553	  return liveFiles[file]
   554	```
   555	
   556	## Minimum-group publication
   557	
   558	A rule cannot count a population. Maintain a count, check its readable group,
   559	and publish a copy atomically. Approvals here are irrevocable, so publication
   560	is monotone. For retractable consent, withdraw the publication in the same
   561	transaction as every withdrawal, as in Materialised access; a later cleanup
   562	job leaves a disclosure window.
   563	
   564	```quarry
   565	table petitions:
   566	  owner: principal
   567	  body: text 1..200
   568	  immutable owner, body
   569	  read <- .owner = viewer
   570	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   571	  online only
   572	table approvals:
   573	  petition: petitions
   574	  principal: principal
   575	  unique petition, principal
   576	  immutable petition, principal
   577	  read <- .principal = viewer or exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
   578	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists petitions[.petition]
   579	  online only
   580	view approvalCount: count approvals by .petition
   581	  read <- exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
   582	table petitionPublications:
   583	  petition: petitions
   584	  body: text 1..200
   585	  unique petition
   586	  immutable petition, body
   587	  public 'the owner published after ten irrevocable approvals'
   588	  insert <- exists petitions[.petition, owner = viewer]
   589	  online only
   590	mutation petition(body: text 1..200):
   591	  return insert petitions { owner: viewer, body }
   592	mutation approve(petition: petitions):
   593	  return insert approvals { petition, principal: viewer }
   594	mutation publishPetition(petition: petitions):
   595	  p = petitions[petition] ! else NOT_FOUND
   596	  require (approvalCount[petition] ?? 0) >= 10 else TOO_FEW
   597	  return insert petitionPublications { petition, body: p.body }
   598	query petitionPublication(petition: petitions):
   599	  return petitionPublications[petition = petition]
   600	```
   601	
   602	Only publish the trusted threshold mutation. The table rule proves the owner,
   603	not the threshold; adding a generic publication writer would bypass it.
   604	
   605	## Secret ballots, closed publication, maintained result
   606	
   607	Keep voter choices private, and disclose only the maintained result after
   608	closing. The public poll metadata makes the closed-phase probe explicit.
   609	Neither insert nor delete can change votes after closure. There is no reopen
   610	operation. A readable empty result is zero; while open the count key is hidden
   611	and reads null. This is choice privacy from other voters, not administrator
   612	anonymity. Small totals may reveal an individual choice.
   613	
   614	```quarry
   615	table polls:
   616	  owner: principal
   617	  closed: bool default false
   618	  immutable owner
   619	  public 'poll identity and whether voting is closed are public'
   620	  insert <- .owner = viewer and .closed = false
   621	  update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer and old.closed = false and next.closed = true
   622	  online only
   623	table ballots:
   624	  poll: polls
   625	  voter: principal
   626	  choice: enum('yes', 'no')
   627	  unique poll, voter
   628	  immutable poll, voter, choice
   629	  read <- .voter = viewer
   630	  insert <- .voter = viewer and exists polls[.poll, closed = false]
   631	  delete <- .voter = viewer and exists polls[.poll, closed = false]
   632	  online only
   633	view ballotResults: count ballots by .poll, .choice
   634	  read <- exists polls[.poll, closed = true]
   635	mutation poll():
   636	  return insert polls { owner: viewer }
   637	mutation vote(poll: polls, choice: enum('yes', 'no')):
   638	  return insert ballots { poll, voter: viewer, choice }
   639	mutation closePoll(poll: polls):
   640	  p = polls[poll] ! else NOT_FOUND
   641	  require p.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER
   642	  update polls[p.id] { closed: true }
   643	  return null
   644	query ballotResult(poll: polls, choice: enum('yes', 'no')):
   645	  return { count: ballotResults[poll, choice] }
   646	query ownBallot(poll: polls):
   647	  return ballots[poll, viewer]
   648	```
   649	
   650	## Ephemeral presence under an enrolment parent
   651	
   652	An ephemeral row cannot use `read through`. Copy the durable scope, verify it
   653	against the writer-readable enrolment on every write, and probe the durable
   654	roster for current read authority. Removing enrolment hides presence on the
   655	next read; the memory row need not be deleted first. Renewals also require
   656	membership. Never replace presence with a durable table to repair this refusal.
   657	
   658	```quarry
   659	table spaces:
   660	  title: text 1..80
   661	  public 'space metadata is public; participation is private'
   662	  online only
   663	table enrolments:
   664	  space: spaces
   665	  principal: principal
   666	  unique space, principal
   667	  immutable space, principal
   668	  read <- .principal = viewer
   669	  insert <- viewer = 'service:ops'
   670	  delete <- .principal = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops'
   671	  online only
   672	table presenceRows:
   673	  enrolment: enrolments
   674	  space: spaces
   675	  principal: principal
   676	  unique space, principal
   677	  immutable enrolment, space, principal
   678	  read <- exists enrolments[.space, viewer]
   679	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer] and .space = .enrolment.space
   680	  update <- .principal = viewer and exists enrolments[.space, viewer] and .space = .enrolment.space
   681	  ephemeral 4s
   682	mutation pulse(space: spaces):
   683	  m = enrolments[space, viewer] ! else NOT_FOUND
   684	  return upsert presenceRows[space, viewer] { enrolment: m.id }
   685	mutation leaveSpace(space: spaces):
   686	  delete enrolments[space, viewer]
   687	  return null
   688	query presence(space: spaces, who: principal):
   689	  return presenceRows[space, who]
   690	```
   691	
   692	## Blind comparison with a system job
   693	
   694	A mutation cannot scan or compare secrets its caller cannot read. A system job
   695	can read them, but must publish only the explicitly permitted derived fact.
   696	Here the operator schedules the comparison, and ordinary callers get only
   697	which of two known submissions is higher, not either score. Jobs run as the
   698	system; no client mutation delegates arbitrary secret comparisons.
   699	
   700	```quarry
   701	table secretScores:
   702	  owner: principal
   703	  score: int
   704	  immutable owner, score
   705	  read <- .owner = viewer
   706	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   707	  online only
   708	table comparisons:
   709	  left: secretScores
   710	  right: secretScores
   711	  leftHigher: bool
   712	  public 'operator-authorized comparison discloses only the ordering'
   713	  online only
   714	mutation secretScore(score: int):
   715	  return insert secretScores { owner: viewer, score }
   716	job compare(left: secretScores, right: secretScores):
   717	  for a in secretScores where .id = left:
   718	    b = secretScores[right] ! else NOT_FOUND
   719	    insert comparisons { left: a.id, right, leftHigher: a.score > b.score }
   720	query comparison(comparison: comparisons):
   721	  return comparisons[comparison]
   722	query secretScoreById(score: secretScores):
   723	  return secretScores[score]
   724	```
   725	
   726	## Enrolment into hidden things: invite, self-join or public metadata
   727	
   728	Choose one entry contract. **Invite:** the inviter reads the private parent and
   729	creates an addressed invitation; the recipient reads that invitation, then
   730	inserts their own membership without looking up the hidden parent. The table
   731	insert rule probes the parent and invitation even when program reads cannot.
   732	**Self-join:** deliberately make the root metadata public and require its open
   733	flag in the membership insert rule. **Public metadata:** keep a separate public
   734	catalogue with no private payload, and let its entry operation create private
   735	membership. Knowing an id is not consent and does not make a hidden parent
   736	readable. `! else NOT_FOUND` before joining will stop the invitee too early.
   737	
   738	```quarry
   739	table hiddenClubs:
   740	  owner: principal
   741	  title: text 1..80
   742	  immutable owner
   743	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists clubMembers[.id, viewer]
   744	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   745	  online only
   746	table clubInvites:
   747	  club: hiddenClubs
   748	  principal: principal
   749	  unique club, principal
   750	  immutable club, principal
   751	  read <- .principal = viewer or exists hiddenClubs[.club, owner = viewer]
   752	  insert <- exists hiddenClubs[.club, owner = viewer]
   753	  delete <- .principal = viewer
   754	  online only
   755	table clubMembers:
   756	  club: hiddenClubs
   757	  principal: principal
   758	  unique club, principal
   759	  immutable club, principal
   760	  read <- .principal = viewer
   761	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists hiddenClubs[.club] and exists clubInvites[.club, viewer]
   762	  online only
   763	mutation club(title: text 1..80):
   764	  return insert hiddenClubs { owner: viewer, title }
   765	mutation inviteClub(club: hiddenClubs, who: principal):
   766	  c = hiddenClubs[club] ! else NOT_FOUND
   767	  return insert clubInvites { club: c.id, principal: who }
   768	mutation acceptClub(club: hiddenClubs):
   769	  i = clubInvites[club, viewer] ! else NOT_FOUND
   770	  m = insert clubMembers { club: i.club, principal: viewer }
   771	  delete clubInvites[i.id]
   772	  return m
   773	query hiddenClub(club: hiddenClubs):
   774	  return hiddenClubs[club]
   775	```
   776	
   777	For the self-join variant, replace the private root read rule with explicit
   778	public metadata and the membership insert rule with `.principal = viewer and
   779	exists root[.root, open = true]`; there is no invite. Do not apply that variant
   780	to a synced closed roster: its grant law also requires existing membership or
   781	a newly created parent (`guide sharing`). SURF owns transport authentication
   782	and production UI recipes; KIT owns proof-kit details in `guide testing`.
   783	
   784	## Revocable media links with expiry
   785	
   786	Redemption copies the link's upper lifetime to a principal grant. A live grant
   787	permits the media row and its download; an expired grant is absent even before
   788	physical sweep. Tokens are unguessable secrets generated outside Quarry. Keep
   789	them out of exported query results and listings. Revoking a token prevents new
   790	redemptions; revoke existing readers separately to remove their media access.
   791	Revocation cannot retract downloaded bytes. The asset itself is placed on the
   792	private row through the ordinary upload API (`guide assets`).
   793	
   794	```quarry
   795	table mediaDocuments:
   796	  owner: principal
   797	  title: text 1..80
   798	  image: image <=5000000 ?
   799	  immutable owner
   800	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists mediaReaders[.id, viewer] or viewer = 'service:ops'

 succeeded in 0ms:
use snapback4_lang::{Diagnostic, compile};
const TABLE: &str = "table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=80\n  unique owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  update <- .owner = viewer\n  online only\n";
fn notes(body: &str) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("{TABLE}{body}"))]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    b.notes
        .into_iter()
        .filter(|n| ["N_WRITE_DENIED", "N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE"].contains(&n.code.as_str()))
        .collect()
}
#[test]
fn require_and_branch_contradictions_for_update_and_upsert() {
    for op in ["update", "upsert"] {
        let b = notes(&format!(
            "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  {op} rows[owner = owner] {{ body: 'x' }}\n"
        ));
        assert_eq!(b.len(), 1, "{b:?}");
        assert_eq!(b[0].code, "N_WRITE_DENIED");
        let b = notes(&format!(
            "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  if owner != viewer:\n    {op} rows[owner = owner] {{ body: 'x' }}\n"
        ));
        assert_eq!(b.len(), 1, "{b:?}");
    }
}
#[test]
fn nullable_point_lookup_dead_branch_is_proved() {
    let b = notes(
        "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  r = rows[owner = owner]\n  if r != null:\n    return r\n  return null\n",
    );
    assert_eq!(b.len(), 1, "{b:?}");
    assert_eq!(b[0].code, "N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE");
}
#[test]
fn row_snapshots_and_scalar_aliases_preserve_the_gate() {
    let b = notes(
        "mutation change(row: rows):\n  r = rows[row] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  who = r.owner\n  require who != viewer else SELF\n  update rows[row] { body: 'x' }\n",
    );
    assert_eq!(b.len(), 1, "{b:?}");
}
#[test]
fn unknown_alternatives_and_invalidation_remain_quiet() {
    for body in [
        "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner = viewer else SELF\n  update rows[owner = owner] { body: 'x' }\n",
        "mutation change(owner: principal, bypass: bool):\n  require owner != viewer or bypass else SELF\n  update rows[owner = owner] { body: 'x' }\n",
        "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  who = owner\n  require who != viewer else SELF\n  who = viewer\n  update rows[owner = who] { body: 'x' }\n",
        "mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  insert rows { owner: viewer, body: 'new' }\n  update rows[owner = owner] { body: 'x' }\n",
        "job change(owner: principal):\n  for r in rows:\n    require owner != viewer else SELF\n    update rows[r.id] { body: 'x' }\n",
    ] {
        assert!(notes(body).is_empty(), "{body}: {:?}", notes(body));
    }
}
#[test]
fn named_images_and_replaced_new_values_are_checked() {
    let source = format!(
        "{}mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  update rows[owner = owner] {{ body: 'x' }}\n",
        TABLE.replace(
            "update <- .owner = viewer",
            "update (old, next) <- old.owner = viewer and next.body = 'x'"
        )
    );
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_WRITE_DENIED"));
    let b = compile(&[(
        "schema.q".into(),
        format!(
            "{}mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  update rows[owner = owner] {{ owner: viewer }}\n",
            TABLE.replace(
                "update <- .owner = viewer",
                "update (old, next) <- next.owner = viewer"
            )
        ),
    )]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(
        b.notes.iter().all(|n| n.code != "N_WRITE_DENIED"),
        "{:?}",
        b.notes
    );
}
#[test]
fn nullable_snapshot_fields_never_prove_physical_row_values() {
    assert!(notes("mutation change(row: rows):\n  r = rows[row]\n  require r.owner = null else NOT_HIDDEN\n  update rows[row] { body: 'x' }\n").is_empty());
}
#[test]
fn notes_predict_the_actual_failed_update_and_null_lookup() {
    use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState};
    let source = format!(
        "{TABLE}mutation change(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  update rows[owner = owner] {{ body: 'x' }}\nmutation inspect(owner: principal):\n  require owner != viewer else SELF\n  r = rows[owner = owner]\n  if r != null:\n    return 'hit'\n  return 'hidden'\n"
    );
    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source)]);
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_WRITE_DENIED"));
    assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE"));
    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
    e.seed(vec![(
        "rows".into(),
        snapback4_core::value::object([
            ("id", Value::Id("r".into())),
            ("owner", Value::Id("alice".into())),
            ("body", Value::String("original".into())),
        ]),
    )])
    .unwrap();
    for viewer in ["bob", "carol", "service:ops"] {
        let c = Context::new(viewer, [("owner".into(), Value::Id("alice".into()))].into()).at(1000);
        assert!(
            matches!(e.mutate("change",None,c.clone()).unwrap().state,WriteState::Failed { why,.. } if why.code=="NOT_FOUND")
        );
        assert!(
            matches!(e.mutate("inspect",None,c).unwrap().state,WriteState::Sent { result:Value::String(s),.. } if s=="hidden")
        );
    }
    assert_eq!(
        e.store().rows("rows").unwrap()[0].field("body").unwrap(),
        &Value::String("original".into())
    );
}

+## Client-visible refusal ladder
+
+For an operation written as decode → asserted lookup → write → require:
+
+| Rung | Client-visible answer |
+| --- | --- |
+| Argument decoding | `E_INPUT`; no statement ran |
+| Hidden or missing `t[id] ! else NOT_FOUND` | `NOT_FOUND`; a nullable `t[id]` is null and does not refuse |
+| Table write rule | `E_RULE` for a readable denied target; `NOT_FOUND` for a hidden update/delete target; hidden insert collisions stay `E_RULE` |
+| Program `require` | The authored `else CODE`, if execution reaches this statement |
+
+After decoding, authored statement order decides precedence: a require placed
+before the write can win, while a later require cannot replace an earlier
+lookup or write refusal. For enrolment without a hidden-parent lookup, see
+`guide rule-recipes --section "Enrolment into hidden things"`. Transport guest
+sessions and production authentication: SURF owns `guide personas`/`guide wire`.
+
 ## Which refusal fires first

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Mutable syntax traversal shared by planning and final index-name resolution.
use crate::ast::*;

pub(crate) fn expression(value: &mut Expr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    match value {
        Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => read_children(read, visit),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. } | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => { expression(left, visit); expression(right, visit); }
        Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => { for item in items { expression(item, visit); } }
        Expr::Object { fields, .. } => { for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } } }
        Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => { expression(base, visit); shape(fields, visit); }
        Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => { expression(then, visit); expression(condition, visit); expression(otherwise, visit); }
        Expr::Walk { seed, .. } => expression(seed, visit),
        Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => {
            for key in keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
            for field in fields { if let Some(value) = &mut field.value { expression(value, visit); } }
            if let Some(value) = condition { expression(value, visit); }
        }
        Expr::Merge(merge) => {
            for value in [&mut merge.matching, &mut merge.filter, &mut merge.cursor].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
            let mut lanes = Vec::new();
            for mut lane in std::mem::take(&mut merge.lanes) {
                if let Some(predicate) = &mut lane.admission { expression(predicate, visit); }
                if let Some((_, read)) = &mut lane.nulls {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    let Expr::Read(planned) = value else { unreachable!("planned null scan") };
                    **read = planned;
                }
                if let Some(value) = &mut lane.distinct { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(fields) = &mut lane.project { shape(fields, visit); }
                for value in &mut lane.prefix { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some((_, value)) = &mut lane.loop_ { expression(value, visit); }
                if let Some(read) = &mut lane.planned {
                    let mut value = Expr::Read(*read.clone());
                    expression(&mut value, visit);
                    match value {
                        Expr::Read(planned) => **read = planned,
                        Expr::Merge(nested) if lane.loop_.is_none() => {
                            for mut child in nested.lanes {
                                child.branch = lane.branch.clone();
                                child.project = lane.project.clone();
                                lanes.push(child);
                            }
                            continue;
                        }
                        _ => unreachable!("a planned lane is a read or an expanded read"),
                    }
                }
                lanes.push(lane);
            }
            merge.lanes = lanes;
        }
        Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => {}
    }
    visit(value);
}

fn read_children(read: &mut ReadExpr, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for key in &mut read.keys { expression(&mut key.value, visit); }
    for seek in &mut read.seeks { expression(&mut seek.value, visit); }
    for value in [&mut read.filter, &mut read.matching].into_iter().flatten() { expression(value, visit); }
    if let Some(value) = read.range.as_mut().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_mut()) { expression(value, visit); }
}

pub(super) fn shape(fields: &mut [ShapeField], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for field in fields { if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { expression(value, visit); } }
}

pub(super) fn statements(body: &mut [Stmt], visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for stmt in body {
        match stmt {
            Stmt::Bind { value, .. } | Stmt::Expr { value, .. } | Stmt::Return { value, .. } => expression(value, visit),
            Stmt::Require { condition, .. } => expression(condition, visit),
            Stmt::Emit { payload, once, .. } => {
                expression(payload, visit);
                if let Some(key) = once { expression(key, visit); }
            }
            Stmt::If { condition, then_body, else_body, .. } => { expression(condition, visit); statements(then_body, visit); statements(else_body, visit); }
            Stmt::As { principal: domain, body, .. } | Stmt::For { domain, body, .. } => { expression(domain, visit); statements(body, visit); }
            Stmt::Break { .. } | Stmt::Continue { .. } => {}
        }
    }
}

pub(crate) fn document(doc: &mut Document, visit: &mut impl FnMut(&mut Expr)) {
    for program in &mut doc.programs { statements(&mut program.prelude, visit); statements(&mut program.body, visit); }
    for function in &mut doc.functions {
        match &mut function.body { FunctionBody::Expression(value) => expression(value, visit), FunctionBody::Block(body) => statements(body, visit) }
    }
    for decl in &mut doc.shapes { shape(&mut decl.fields, visit); }
}

   853	
   854	Only expose claimMedia as the reader writer. A generic insert of a grant would
   855	bypass possession and expiry checks. Repeated claims use the declared unique
   856	constraint; they do not extend a grant's expiry.
   857	
   858	## N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE
   859	
   860	N_LOOKUP_NEVER_VISIBLE means a nullable point lookup is always null for callers
   861	satisfying its preceding require/branch conditions: those conditions contradict
   862	the table read rule, so a non-null branch cannot run. Authorize the read or
   863	write by key under the table rule; Enrolment into hidden things shows the latter.
   864	Unknown relationships and role overlap do not trigger this proof.
   865	
   866	### Self-join through a public catalogue
   867	
   868	The public catalogue is the root metadata; the private payload has a separate
   869	row rule. Open entries permit self-join without an invitation. Closed entries
   870	refuse the join even though their metadata is readable. Opening an entry is
   871	an explicit owner decision, not a consequence of knowing its id.
   872	
   873	```quarry
   874	table clubCatalogue:
   875	  owner: principal
   876	  title: text 1..80
   877	  open: bool
   878	  immutable owner
   879	  public 'club titles and whether enrolment is open are public metadata'
   880	  insert <- .owner = viewer
   881	  update <- .owner = viewer
   882	  online only
   883	table catalogueMembers:
   884	  club: clubCatalogue
   885	  principal: principal
   886	  unique club, principal
   887	  immutable club, principal
   888	  read <- .principal = viewer
   889	  insert <- .principal = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, open = true]
   890	  online only
   891	table clubSheets:
   892	  club: clubCatalogue
   893	  owner: principal
   894	  body: text 1..200
   895	  unique club
   896	  immutable club, owner
   897	  read <- .owner = viewer or exists catalogueMembers[.club, viewer]
   898	  insert <- .owner = viewer and exists clubCatalogue[.club, owner = .owner]
   899	  online only
   900	mutation catalogueClub(title: text 1..80, open: bool, body: text 1..200):
   901	  c = insert clubCatalogue { owner: viewer, title, open }
   902	  insert clubSheets { club: c.id, owner: viewer, body }
   903	  return c
   904	mutation selfJoinClub(club: clubCatalogue):
   905	  return insert catalogueMembers { club, principal: viewer }
   906	query catalogueClubById(club: clubCatalogue):
   907	  return clubCatalogue[club]
   908	query clubSheet(club: clubCatalogue):
   909	  return clubSheets[club = club]
