/// `{ column: value }` a read pins, its keys resolved against the table's
/// indexes (named keys, or positional keys of the one unique index of that
/// width) and its `where` equalities. None when a positional key is ambiguous.
pub(super) fn pins(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<BTreeMap<String, Expr>> {
    let info = catalog.tables.get(&read.table)?;
    let mut out = BTreeMap::new();
    let positional: Vec<&KeyPart> = read.keys.iter().filter(|k| k.column.is_none()).collect();
    if !positional.is_empty() {
        let columns = match read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name)) {
            Some(index) => index.columns.clone(),
            None => {
                let unique: Vec<&IndexDecl> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() && !index.columns.iter().any(|c| c == "id")).collect();
                match unique.as_slice() { [index] => index.columns.clone(), _ => return None }
            }
        };
        if columns.len() < read.keys.len() { return None; }
        for (column, key) in columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) { out.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone()); }
    } else {
        for key in &read.keys { out.insert(key.column.clone()?, key.value.clone()); }
    }
    let mut filters = Vec::new();
    if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
    for filter in filters {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
            && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { out.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
    }
    Some(out)
}

/// Every `exists` a condition asserts (outside `not`): `a and exists t[…]`, either side of `or`.
fn asserted_probes<'a>(condition: &'a Expr, out: &mut Vec<&'a ReadExpr>) {
    match condition {
        Expr::Exists { read, .. } => out.push(read),
        Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "and" || op == "or" => { asserted_probes(left, out); asserted_probes(right, out); }
        _ => {}
    }
}

fn requires(body: &[Stmt], out: &mut Vec<(Expr, String)>) {
    for statement in body {
        match statement {
            Stmt::Require { condition, code, .. } => out.push((condition.clone(), code.clone())),
            Stmt::If { then_body, else_body, .. } => { requires(then_body, out); requires(else_body, out); }
            Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => requires(body, out),
            _ => {}
        }
    }
}

fn hidden_requires(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, programs: &[&ProgramDecl], notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
    for program in programs {
        let mut tested = Vec::new();
        requires(&program.prelude, &mut tested);
        requires(&program.body, &mut tested);
        for (condition, code) in &tested {
            let mut probes = Vec::new();
            asserted_probes(condition, &mut probes);
            for read in probes {
                let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { continue };
                let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { continue };
                if table.through.is_some() || info.maintained.is_some() || table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable() { continue; }
                let Some(pinned) = pins(catalog, read) else { continue };
                // Every read disjunct admits only the row whose pinned `column` is the caller,
                // and the probe pins that column to something else.
                let owners: Vec<Option<(String, String)>> = table.rules.read.disjuncts().into_iter().map(|disjunct| conjuncts(disjunct).into_iter().find_map(|c| match c {
                    RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(column), RuleTerm::Viewer) | RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Field(column)) =>
                        pinned.get(column).filter(|value| !is_viewer(value)).map(|value| (column.clone(), crate::print::expression(value))),
                    _ => None,
                })).collect();
                if owners.is_empty() || owners.iter().any(Option::is_none) { continue; }
                let Some((column, value)) = owners[0].clone() else { continue };
                if !seen.insert((program.name.clone(), read.site.to_string())) { continue; }
                let probe = crate::print::expression(&Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: read.site.clone() });
                let site = rule_site(catalog, &read.table, Effect::Read).map(|s| format!(" ({s})")).unwrap_or_default();
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",
                    format!("{} {}: `{probe}` reads {} under its read rule `{}`{site}, which admits only the row whose {column} is the caller, so this require passes only when `{value}` is the caller: for every other {} row, which exists but is hidden, it refuses {code} (a program `exists` sees only readable rows; a rule probe sees every row)",
                        if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query { "query" } else { "mutation" }, program.name, read.table, table.rules.read.quarry(), read.table),
                    format!("let the callers who run this check read those rows: `read <- {} or exists {}[viewer]` (a roster its members read), or a role (`… or exists staff[viewer]`); if only the caller's own row is meant, write `{column} = viewer`", table.rules.read.quarry(), read.table)).guide("rules"));
            }
        }
    }
}

// ---- N_REQUIRE_REFUSED (wave 7, lane 072) ----

/// A program condition, rewritten as a rule over the row `delete t[key]` removes:
/// a name the key pins becomes `.column`, a lookup of that row (`m = t[key]`)
/// its columns, and `y.c = viewer` on a lookup `y = u[e]` of a row `e` the key
/// pins becomes the probe `exists u[.column, c = viewer]`. None for anything else.
struct Row<'a> {
    table: &'a str,
    pinned: BTreeMap<String, String>,
    rows: BTreeSet<String>,
    lookups: BTreeMap<String, (String, String)>,

 succeeded in 0ms:
//! Compiled examples first; diagnostic details are under `why`.
pub(super) const CARD: &str = r##"# Programs

Queries read data; mutations change it in one transaction.
Jobs run background work, and helpers share logic between programs.

```quarry
query programForms(roomId: rooms):                             -- reads only; the last expression, or `return`, is the result, typed onto api.ts
  rooms[roomId] card

mutation programWrites(roomId: rooms, title: text 1..80):     -- reads and writes in one transaction, at most once per client-minted id
  room = rooms[roomId] ! else NOT_FOUND
  require room.ownerId = viewer else NOT_OWNER
  update rooms[room] { name: title }                           -- a final write is the implicit return
```

`programForms` returns the `card` shape; `programWrites` checks visibility
and ownership before renaming.
Helpers share one namespace; a helper from `m.q` accepts both `helper(x)` and `m.helper(x)`.
A different module name is refused with the declaring file and the correct call.
Helpers inherit loop placement (guide jobs); repeated pure calls narrow (guide types).

## Limits

- Queries cannot write or mint ids, including through helpers: E_QUARRY_PLACEMENT.
- Mutation scans need an authorized scope: E_STRUCTURAL_FANOUT.
- Application refusal codes cannot start with `E_`: E_OP_RESERVED.

## Details

Queries read data; mutations change it in one transaction.
Jobs run background work, and helpers share logic between programs.

### Loop writes

Read an authorized page, then write each row:
Mutation scans of expiring tables require `predicts: "never"` —
inferred from server-only work, or declared with `: server` — and must sit in
the initial read-only phase. A write or emit anywhere in an enclosing statement
ends it. Bind before writing loops:

```quarry
table expiringNotes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  expiresAt: time ?
  read <- .owner = viewer
  update <- .owner = viewer
  expire at .expiresAt
  sync to .owner last 100 by .id

mutation annotateNotes(): server
  page = expiringNotes where .owner = viewer first 10 by .id
  for row in page:
    update expiringNotes[row] { body: 'read' }
  return null
```

Pages still need uniform plain prefixes; unswept expired rows cost no meter
(guide bounds).

### App files and modules

```
my-app/
  snapback/schema.q          the backend: tables, rules, sync, queries, mutations
  snapback/workers/push.mjs  declared Node worker; dev and serve start it (guide effects: ledger: true)
  snapback/worker.mts        optional single Node ESM worker: effects and actions (dev starts it)
  snapback/generated/package.json  ESM boundary for generated artifacts
  snapback/generated/api.ts  written by `check`; never edited, never committed
  src/                       your client: React on the web, React Native on Expo
```

A backend shares one namespace of tables, shapes and helpers across `.q`
files. `snapback/schema.q` supplies root operations; `snapback/social.q`
supplies `api.social.<op>`. Import the generated `api` and use one client.
`sync` declares which devices hold rows and answer local reads; there are
no resolvers, migrations or subscriptions to manage.

**Two ways to run.** `snapback4 init` starts local-first: declare `sync` and
pass a device to `createApp` for offline data. `init --online` creates
`src/client.ts` without a device or `sync`; server queries stay live through
`/changes`. Both share rules, meters, identity, assets and jobs. Add a device
and `sync` later. `init --expo` selects native when available, online in Expo Go.
Starters, `check` and `dev` write `snapback/generated/package.json` with
`{"type":"module"}`: Node ESM seeds can import `api.ts` even in a CommonJS
app. Other scripts retain their module interpretation; Expo's `worker.mts`
explicitly uses ESM.

"##;

fn column_ty(info: &TableInfo, name: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    if name == "id" {
        return Some(Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()));
    }
    info.columns.get(name).or_else(|| info.captures.get(name)).map(Ty::from_scalar)
}

fn lexical_value(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
    match expr {
        Expr::Variable(_) => true,
        Expr::Field { base, .. } => lexical_value(base),
        _ => false,
    }
}

pub(super) fn allocates(expr: &Expr) -> bool {
    match expr {
        Expr::Input(Input::NewId) => true,
        Expr::Array(values) | Expr::Builtin { args: values, .. } => values.iter().any(allocates),
        Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().any(allocates),
        Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => allocates(left) || allocates(right),
        Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Field { base: value, .. } => allocates(value),
        _ => false,
    }
}

/// Only a named row or focal row can head a literal aggregate column chain.
fn aggregate_path(expr: &A) -> Option<(&A, String)> {
    let mut root = expr;
    let mut fields = Vec::new();
    while let A::Member { base, name, .. } = root { fields.push(name.as_str()); root = base; }
    if fields.len() < 2 || !matches!(root, A::Focal { .. } | A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
    fields.reverse();
    Some((root, fields.join(".")))
}

//! Programs, chunks and schedules: `query`, `mutation`, `job` (Quarry
//! chunks or `native`, LLP 3000.005; a query may be `native` too, §9) and `schedule`. Split from the parser
//! so each file stays under the line budget.

use super::Parser;
use crate::ast::*;
use crate::diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, syntax, syntax_with, unsupported};
use crate::lexer::Kind;

impl Parser {
    /// `schedule name every <duration> [at HH:MM]: job`, at least one minute,
    /// or `every N month[s]|year[s] [at [day D] HH:MM]`.
    pub(super) fn parse_schedule(&mut self) -> Fallible<ScheduleDecl> {
        let site = self.take("schedule")?.site;
        let rewrite = "schedule digest every 1d at 08:00: sendDigests";
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        if !self.maybe("every") {
            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a schedule fires every so often: schedule name every 2m: job", rewrite));
        }
        let amount = self.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
        let unit = self.word()?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.040#24-monthly-and-yearly-schedules — calendar months and years, UTC, anchored at 1970-01.
        let calendar = match unit.value.as_str() { "month" | "months" => Some(1), "year" | "years" => Some(12), _ => None };
        if let Some(per) = calendar {
            let rewrite = "schedule billing every 1 month at day 1 09:00: bill";
            let months = amount.value.parse::<u32>().ok().filter(|n| *n >= 1).and_then(|n| n.checked_mul(per))
                .filter(|n| *n <= snapback4_core::schema::CALENDAR_MONTHS_LIMIT)
                .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&amount.site, "a calendar schedule fires every 1 to 1200 months (100 years)", rewrite).guide("jobs"))?;
            let mut offset_ms = 0;
            if self.maybe("at") {
                let mut day = 1;
                if self.maybe("day") {
                    let token = self.peek().clone();
                    day = self.number()?;
                    if !(1..=28).contains(&day) {
                        return Err(syntax_with(&token.site, format!("day {day} is not in every month; a calendar schedule's day is 1 through 28"),
                            "at day 28 09:00, or a daily schedule whose job tests month(periodStart) != month(periodEnd): that daily period crosses a month's end").guide("jobs"));
                    }
                }
                let hours = self.number()?;
                self.take(":")?;
                let minutes = self.number()?;
                if hours > 23 || minutes > 59 {
                    return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at takes a day of the month and a time of day: at day 1 09:00", rewrite));
                }
                offset_ms = (u64::from(day - 1) * 86_400 + u64::from(hours) * 3_600 + u64::from(minutes) * 60) * 1_000;
            }
            return self.schedule_tail(name, 0, offset_ms, months, site);
        }
        let multiplier = super::views::duration_unit(&unit.value).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&unit.site,
            "a schedule period is a whole number followed by ms, s, m, h or d, or a number of months or years", "every 2m, every 1d, or every 1 month"))?;
        // Integer millisecond boundaries need no rounding to whole minutes.
        // Retain the range of previously accepted m/h/d schedule periods.
        let every_ms = amount.value.parse::<i64>().ok().and_then(|value| value.checked_mul(multiplier))
            .ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&amount.site, "schedule period exceeds the representable millisecond range", "every 1d"))? as u64;
        if every_ms < 60_000 {
            return Err(syntax_with(&amount.site, "a schedule fires at most once a minute", "every 1m"));
        }
        let mut offset_ms = 0;
        if self.maybe("at") {
            if self.at("day") {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at day D names a day of a calendar month; it needs every 1 month (or N months or years)", "schedule billing every 1 month at day 1 09:00: bill").guide("jobs"));
            }
            let hours = self.number()?;
            self.take(":")?;
            let minutes = self.number()?;
            if hours > 23 || minutes > 59 {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at takes a time of day, HH:MM", rewrite));
            }
            offset_ms = (u64::from(hours) * 60 + u64::from(minutes)) * 60_000;
            if offset_ms >= every_ms {
                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "at names a time of day inside the period; it needs every 1d (or longer)", rewrite));
            }
        }
        self.schedule_tail(name, every_ms, offset_ms, 0, site)
    }

    fn schedule_tail(&mut self, name: String, every_ms: u64, offset_ms: u64, months: u32, site: crate::Site) -> Fallible<ScheduleDecl> {
        let service = if self.maybe("as") {
            let namespace = self.word()?;
            if namespace.value != "service" {
                return Err(syntax_with(&namespace.site, "a scheduled action runs as a service seat", "as service:billing").guide("actions"));
            }
            self.take(":")?;
            let mut name = self.service_name_part()?;
            while self.maybe("-") {
                name.push('-');
                name.push_str(&self.service_name_part()?);
            }
            Some(format!("service:{name}"))
        } else { None };
        self.take(":")?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#10 — module jobs use the same qualified name in schedules as in the compiled program.
        let mut target = self.word()?.value;
        if self.maybe(".") { target.push('.'); target.push_str(&self.word()?.value); }
        self.end_line(&site)?;
        Ok(ScheduleDecl { name, every_ms, offset_ms, target, service, months, site })
    }

    fn service_name_part(&mut self) -> Fallible<String> {
        if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { Ok(self.take_kind(Kind::Number)?.value) } else { Ok(self.word()?.value) }
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_program(&mut self) -> Fallible<ProgramDecl> {
        let kind_token = self.word()?;
        let kind = match kind_token.value.as_str() {
            "query" => ProgramKind::Query,
            "mutation" => ProgramKind::Mutation,
            _ => ProgramKind::Job,
        };
        let name = self.binding_name()?;
        if kind == ProgramKind::Job && self.at("every") {
            let mut period = String::new();
            let mut end = 0;
            while !matches!(self.peek().kind, Kind::Newline | Kind::Eof) {
                let token = self.peek().clone();
                if !period.is_empty() && token.site.col > end { period.push(' '); }
                period.push_str(&token.value);
                end = token.site.col + token.value.len() as u32;
                self.position += 1;
            }
            let period = period.trim_end_matches(':').trim_end();
            let module = std::path::Path::new(&kind_token.site.file).file_stem().and_then(|stem| stem.to_str()).unwrap_or("schema");
            let target = if module == "schema" { name.clone() } else { format!("{module}.{name}") };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&kind_token.site, "E_QUARRY_JOB_SCHEDULE",
                format!("job {name} declares work; a separate schedule declares when it runs"),
                format!("job {name}():  -- keep the existing indented body\nschedule {name}Every {period}: {target}")).guide("jobs"));
        }
        if self.at(":") {
            return Err(syntax_with(&kind_token.site, "an operation with no arguments still needs parentheses", format!("{} {name}():", kind_token.value)).mechanical());
        }
        let args_site = self.peek().site.clone();
        let args = self.parse_args()?;
        self.repair_args = Some((args_site, args.clone()));
        if self.at("->") {
            return Err(unsupported(&self.peek().site.clone(), "result annotations", "results are inferred; remove the annotation"));
        }
        let held = kind == ProgramKind::Query && self.maybe("held");
        let predicts = kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && self.maybe("predicts");
        // A clause may continue the header on the body's indentation level.
        let continued = self.at_kind(Kind::Newline) && self.peek_at(1).kind == Kind::Indent && self.peek_at(2).value == "reports";
        if continued { self.newline()?; self.take_kind(Kind::Indent)?; }
        let delivery = if self.at("reports") { Some(self.parse_delivery()?) } else { None };
        if delivery.is_some() && !self.at(":") {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&self.peek().site, "E_DELIVERY_CLAUSE",
                "a mutation header has one delivery clause, followed by its body colon",
                "keep one reports delivery of messages by .conversation, .ordinal: clause").guide("receipts"));
        }
        self.take(":")?;
        if self.at("native") {
            // `job name(): native` — the body is the app's Rust (LLP 3000.005).
            let native = self.take("native")?;
            self.end_line(&native.site)?;
            if kind == ProgramKind::Mutation {
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&native.site, "E_NATIVE", format!("only a job or a query can be native; a mutation runs in the interpreter so a device can predict it and the server can replay it"), format!("job {name}(): native  -- or: query {name}(): native")).guide("native"));
            }
            return Ok(ProgramDecl { delivery, held, predicts, server: false, kind, name, args, prelude: Vec::new(), body: Vec::new(), native: true, site: kind_token.site });
        }
        let server = kind == ProgramKind::Mutation && self.maybe("server");
        self.newline()?;
        let before_body = self.diagnostics.len();
        let (prelude, body) = if kind == ProgramKind::Job { self.parse_chunks(&args)? } else { (Vec::new(), if continued { self.parse_block_contents()? } else { self.parse_block()? }) };
        if body.is_empty() {
            // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.19 — default `check` hides a recovery diagnostic only
            // when an earlier error in the same operation's own body already explains it.
            // A job's parsed prelude cannot explain missing chunks; that repair is independent.
            if kind == ProgramKind::Job { return Err(job_without_sweep(&kind_token.site, &name, &prelude)); }
            let mut diagnostic = syntax(&kind_token.site, format!("{} {name} has an empty body", kind_token.value));
            diagnostic.cascade_of = self.diagnostics[before_body..].iter().find(|d| d.code.starts_with("E_")).map(|d| d.site.clone());
            return Err(diagnostic);
        }
        Ok(ProgramDecl { delivery, held, predicts, server, kind, name, args, prelude, body, native: false, site: kind_token.site })
    }

    fn parse_delivery(&mut self) -> Fallible<DeliveryDecl> {
        let site = self.take("reports")?.site;
        let parsed = (|| {
            self.take("delivery")?;
            self.take("of")?;
            let table = self.word()?.value;
            self.take("by")?;
            self.take(".")?;
            let group = self.word()?.value;
            self.take(",")?;
            self.take(".")?;
            let position = self.word()?.value;
            Ok(DeliveryDecl { table, group, position, site: site.clone() })
        })();
        parsed.map_err(|_: Diagnostic| Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_DELIVERY_CLAUSE",
            "a delivery clause names one table and its group and position columns",
            "mutation delivered(conversation: conversations, from: int, through: int) reports delivery of messages by .conversation, .ordinal:").guide("receipts"))
    }

    pub(super) fn parse_chunks(&mut self, args: &[ArgDecl]) -> Fallible<(Vec<Stmt>, Vec<Stmt>)> {

        while !self.at_kind(Kind::Dedent) && !self.at_kind(Kind::Eof) {
            self.skip_newlines();

pub fn bind(documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut bound = bind_unverified(documents.clone());
    crate::repair::verify_bound(&documents, &mut bound.diagnostics);
    bound
}

// Repair checks run the same planner and admission, without recursively
// verifying the diagnostics from a rejected candidate.
pub(crate) fn bind_unverified(mut documents: Vec<Document>) -> Bound {
    let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — catalog provenance comes from source, before any generated declarations.
    let authored_indexes = documents.iter().flat_map(|doc|
        doc.tables.iter().map(|t| (&t.name, &t.indexes))
            .chain(doc.maintains.iter().map(|m| (&m.name, &m.indexes))))
        .map(|(name, indexes)| (name.clone(), indexes.iter().map(|i| i.name.clone()).collect())).collect();
    receipts::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics);
    helper_names::normalize(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let server = documents.iter().any(|doc| doc.server);
    server::admit(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let feeds = feed_view::expand(&mut documents, &mut diagnostics);
    // Following expansion adds its durable timeline inputs. Recheck before lowering.
    if !feeds.is_empty() { ephemeral::declarations(&documents, &mut diagnostics); }
    let documents = timeline::expand(documents, &mut diagnostics);
    let personas: Vec<String> = documents.iter().flat_map(|d| d.personas.iter().cloned()).collect();
    let mut catalog = Catalog::build(documents, &mut diagnostics);
    catalog.feed_views = feeds;
    page_shapes::hoist(&mut catalog);
    mixed_keys::normalize(&mut catalog);
    intent::table_order(&mut catalog);
    intent_aggregates::remember_domains(&mut catalog);
    intent_search::capture(&mut catalog);
    intent_variants::expand(&mut catalog);
    intent_views::expand(&mut catalog, &mut diagnostics);
    // After argument-presence variants: `x = null or …` stays theirs (cursor validation across variants).
    where_guards::lower(&mut catalog);
    let stable_fields = catalog.tables.iter().map(|(name, table)| {
        let mut fields: std::collections::BTreeSet<String> = table.decl.columns.iter()
            .filter(|column| column.written_by.is_none() && column.maintained.is_none()).map(|column| column.name.clone()).collect();
        fields.insert("id".into()); (name.clone(), fields)
    }).collect();
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents { for program in &mut doc.programs { stable_page_keys::lower_program(program, &stable_fields); } }
    intent::plan(&mut catalog, &mut diagnostics);
    reference_actions::indexes(&mut catalog);
    let mut schema = schema::bind_schema(&catalog, &mut diagnostics);
    schema.authored_indexes = snapback4_core::schema::inspection::AuthoredIndexes(Some(authored_indexes));
    timeline::admit(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
    delivery::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
    receipts::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
    schema.prepare_receipt_dependencies();
    reference_actions::bind(&catalog, &mut schema, &mut diagnostics);
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#1 — timeline mappings exist only after lowering both spellings.
    for issue in snapback4_core::schema::field_authority::validate(&schema) {
        if !diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY" && d.message == issue.message) {
            diagnostics.push(crate::diag::Diagnostic::new(&catalog.site_for(&issue.path), "E_FIELD_AUTHORITY", issue.message,
                "declare readable by audience before using a private field in a timeline mapping or predicate").guide("rules"));
        }
    }
    // Timeline ownership is resolved after the ordinary schema checks.

codex
NOT READY

- **[P2] Invalidate snapshots before evaluating effectful `if` conditions.** [rule_composition.rs:233](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/rule_composition.rs:233) enters branches with existing facts before reaching the effect scan at line 274. A helper in the condition can therefore change the witnessed row without invalidating its snapshot. For example, with public reads and `update (old, next) <- next.owner = viewer`, assert a row, require `r.owner != viewer`, then call a condition helper that sets its owner to `viewer` and returns true. A subsequent body update succeeds, but this analysis emits `N_WRITE_DENIED` using the stale owner. Scan condition effects and clear evidence before analyzing either branch.

- **[P2] Normalize numeric literals before proving they differ.** [rule_composition.rs:80](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/rule_composition.rs:80) identifies literals by AST debug text; lines 51–56 treat different spellings as distinct constants. Quarry instead parses numeric literals as integers ([bind/mod.rs:479](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs:479)). Consequently, an asserted row followed by `require r.level = 01` and an update governed by `.level = 1` produces a false contradiction although both values equal integer 1. Canonicalize numeric terms through the binder’s integer conversion before equality analysis.

- **[P2] Invalidate snapshots before evaluating effectful `if` conditions.** [rule_composition.rs:233](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/rule_composition.rs:233) enters branches with existing facts before reaching the effect scan at line 274. A helper in the condition can therefore change the witnessed row without invalidating its snapshot. For example, with public reads and `update (old, next) <- next.owner = viewer`, assert a row, require `r.owner != viewer`, then call a condition helper that sets its owner to `viewer` and returns true. A subsequent body update succeeds, but this analysis emits `N_WRITE_DENIED` using the stale owner. Scan condition effects and clear evidence before analyzing either branch.

- **[P2] Normalize numeric literals before proving they differ.** [rule_composition.rs:80](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/rule_composition.rs:80) identifies literals by AST debug text; lines 51–56 treat different spellings as distinct constants. Quarry instead parses numeric literals as integers ([bind/mod.rs:479](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-w12-rules2/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs:479)). Consequently, an asserted row followed by `require r.level = 01` and an update governed by `.level = 1` produces a false contradiction although both values equal integer 1. Canonicalize numeric terms through the binder’s integer conversion before equality analysis.
